\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}{{\binampersand}}A~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxType}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxTypePsome})}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x(){(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})};Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta,x{:}{\oplus}\overline{A}~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxType}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxTypeGsome})}}{(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x(){(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})};Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}\implies\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxRedOne}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxRedOnePsome})}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta,x{:}\overline{A}~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxRedOne}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxRedOneGsome})}}{(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathtt{none}}]
.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.\mathtt{none}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}{{\binampersand}}A~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxType}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxTypePnone})}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x(){(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})};Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta,x{:}{\oplus}\overline{A}~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxType}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxTypeGsome})}}{(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.\mathtt{none}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x(){(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})};Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}\implies\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\bm{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxRedOne}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxRedOnePnone})}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{w_{1}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\ldots\mathbin{|}\overline{w_{n}}.\mathtt{none}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxRedOne}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxRedOneGsome})}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\bm{0}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{w_{1}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\ldots\mathbin{|}\overline{w_{n}}.\mathtt{none}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{{?}{!}}]
.

\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{?}\overline{x}[y];P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}{?}A~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxType}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxTypePuname})}\quad{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{!}x(z);Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta,x{:}{!}\overline{A}~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxType}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxTypeGuname})}}{(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{?}\overline{x}[y];P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{!}x(y);Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}\implies\frac{}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}\big[(\bm{\nu}x)((\bm{\nu}y)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}Q\{y/z\})\mathbin{|}{!}x(z);Q)\big]\vdash\Gamma,\Delta~~\text{(\lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{l:ctxRedOne}.\lx@cref{refnum}{i:ctxRedOneUname})}}

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\equiv}]
.
Assume
P\equiv P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}\equiv Q
.
By
Theorem

0.E.1
,
P^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma
.
By the IH,
Q^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma
.
By
Theorem

0.E.1
,
Q\vdash\Gamma
.

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\nu}]
.
Assume
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime}
.

\frac{P\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A\quad Q\vdash\Delta,x{:}\overline{A}}{(\bm{\nu}x)(P\mathbin{|}Q)\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}\implies\frac{P^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A~~\text{(IH)}\quad Q\vdash\Delta,x{:}\overline{A}}{(\bm{\nu}x)(P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}Q)\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{|}}]
.
Assume
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime}
.

\frac{P\vdash\Gamma\quad Q\vdash\Delta}{P\mathbin{|}Q\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}\implies\frac{P^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma~~\text{(IH)}\quad Q\vdash\Delta}{P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}Q\vdash\Gamma,\Delta}

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}}]
.
Assume
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}P^{\prime}
.

\frac{P\vdash\Gamma\quad Q\vdash\Gamma}{P\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q\vdash\Gamma}\implies\frac{P^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma~~\text{(IH)}\quad Q\vdash\Gamma}{P^{\prime}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q\vdash\Gamma}

∎

Deadlock-freedom

The proof uses several definitions and lemmas, which we summarize:

•

Definition

13
defines single-choice multi-hole contexts, where holes may only appear on one side of non-deterministic choices.

Definition

14
yields deterministic multi-hole contexts from single-choice multi-hole contexts by committing non-deterministic choices to the sides of holes.

Lemma

4
ensures typing remains consistent when committing a single-choice multi-hole context.

•

Lemma

5
states that any typable process not equivalent to
\bm{0}
can be written as an S-context with each hole replaced by a prefixed process.
Let us refer to this as the
S-context form
.

•

Lemma

6
states that if a process in S-context form is typable under empty context and has a forwarder as one of its prefixes, that process contains a cut on one of the forwarder’s subjects.

•

Lemma

7
states that the number of prefixed processes of a process in S-context form is at least the number of cuts in the S-context.
This lemma is key to the proof of Deadlock Freedom, as it is necessary to show the next lemma.

•

Lemma

8
states that if a process in S-context form is typable under empty context, then there must be two of its prefixed processes that share a subject.

Definition 13

(Single-choice Multi-hole Contexts)
.

We define
single-choice multi-hole contexts
(S-contexts, for short) as follows:

{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}::={[\cdot]}_{i}\mathbin{\scalebox{1.3}{$\mid$}}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}})\mathbin{\scalebox{1.3}{$\mid$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{\scalebox{1.3}{$\mid$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}P

An S-context is
n
-ary if it has
n
holes
{[\cdot]}_{1},\ldots,{[\cdot]}_{n}
.
We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P_{1},\ldots,P_{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
to denote the process obtained from the
n
-ary multi-hole context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
by replacing each
i
-th hole in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
with
P_{i}
.
Given an S-context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
with hole indices
I
and a sequence of processes
{(P_{i})}_{i\in I}
, we write
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
to denote the process obtained from
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
by replacing each hole with index
i
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
with
P_{i}
.
We say an S-context is a
deterministic multi-hole context
(DM-context, for short) if its holes do not appear inside any non-deterministic choices.

Definition 14

(Commitment of Single-choice Multi-hole Contexts)
.

We define the
commitment
of S-context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
, by abuse of notation denoted
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}
(cf.
Section

5
), as follows, yielding a deterministic multi-hole context:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{[\cdot]}_{i}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}

\displaystyle:={[\cdot]}_{i}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}^{\prime})\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}

\displaystyle:=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}^{\prime}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}P\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}

\displaystyle:={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}

Lemma 4

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}\vdash\Gamma
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}[P_{i}]_{i\in I}\vdash\Gamma
.

Proof.

Straightforward, by induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
.
∎

Lemma 5

If
P\vdash\Gamma
and
P\not\equiv\bm{0}
, then there exist S-context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
with indices
I
and sequence of prefixed processes
{(\alpha_{i};P_{i})}_{i\in I}
such that
P\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
.

Proof.

Using structural congruence, we first remove all cuts with unused servers and parallel compositions with
\bm{0}
, obtaining
P^{\prime}\equiv P
.
Since
P\not\equiv\bm{0}
,
P^{\prime}\not\equiv\bm{0}
.
Then, we construct
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
by induction on the typing derivation of
P^{\prime}
.
Rules
[
Tempty
]
and
[
Tweaken
]
do not occur, because of how we obtained
P^{\prime}
from
P
.
The structural rules
[
Tmix
]
,
[
Tcut
]
, and
[
Tweaken
]
are simply copied.
In case of rule
[
T
\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}
]
, we arbitrarily pick a branch to continue the construction of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
with, while copying the entire other branch.
The other rules, which type prefixes, add a hole to
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
; we mark the hole with index
i
and refer to the prefixed process typed by the rule as
\alpha_{i};P_{i}
.
Clearly,
P\equiv P^{\prime}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
.
∎

Lemma 6

If
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}\vdash\emptyset
and there is
j\in I
s.t.
\alpha_{j}=[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
, then there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}},Q
such that
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}
.

Proof.

Note that there must be a restriction on
x
in
P
, because
x
appears free in
[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
but
P\vdash\emptyset
.
First, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}
from
P
by replacing the restriction on
x
in
P
with a hole, referring the parallel component in which
[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
appears as
P^{\prime}
and the other parallel component as
Q
.
Then, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}
from
P^{\prime}
by replacing
[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
with a hole.
Clearly,
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}
.
∎

Lemma 7

If the derivation of
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}\vdash\Gamma
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
is deterministic and contains
n
cuts, then
|I|\geq n+1
.

Proof.

We apply strong induction on the number
n
of cuts in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
:

•

Case
n=0
.
Any S-context must have at least one hole, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
has at least one hole.
Hence,
|I|\geq 1=n+1
.

•

Case
n=n^{\prime}+1
.
By abuse of notation,
P=P_{1}\mathbin{|}\ldots\mathbin{|}P_{k}
, where for each
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
,
P_{k^{\prime}}
is not a parallel composition.
By assumption,
m\geq 1
of the
P_{1},\ldots,P_{k}
are cuts.
W.l.o.g., assume
P_{1},\ldots,P_{m}
are cuts.

For each
1\leq j\leq m
, by inversion of rule
[
Tmix
]
,
P_{j}\vdash\Gamma_{j}
, and by construction, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S_{j}}},I_{j}
s.t.
P_{j}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I_{j}}
where
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S_{j}}}
is deterministic.
We have for each
1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq m
where
j\neq j^{\prime}
that
I_{j}\mathbin{\#}I_{j^{\prime}}
, and
{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{1\leq j\leq m}I_{j}\subseteq I
.
Then, for each
1\leq j\leq m
, let
1\leq n_{j}\leq n
be the number of cuts in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S_{j}}}
.
Since
P_{m+1},\ldots,P_{k}
are not cuts, we have
{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\sum}_{1\leq j\leq m}n_{j}=n
.

Take any
1\leq j\leq m
.
We have
P_{j}=(\bm{\nu}x)(P^{\prime}_{j}\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{j})
, and by inversion of rule
[
Tcut
]
,
P^{\prime}_{j}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime}_{j},x{:}A
and
P^{\prime\prime}_{j}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{j},x{:}\overline{A}
where
\Gamma_{j}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{j},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{j}
.
By construction, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime}_{j}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime\prime}_{j}}},I^{\prime}_{j},I^{\prime\prime}_{j}
s.t.
P^{\prime}_{j}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime}_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I^{\prime}_{j}}
and
P^{\prime\prime}_{j}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime\prime}_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I^{\prime\prime}_{j}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime}_{j}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime\prime}_{j}}}
are deterministic.
We have
I^{\prime}_{j}\mathbin{\#}I^{\prime\prime}_{j}
and
I^{\prime}_{j}\cup I^{\prime\prime}_{j}=I_{j}
.

Let
n^{\prime}_{j}
and
n^{\prime\prime}_{j}
be the number of cuts in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime}_{j}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S^{\prime\prime}_{j}}}
, respectively.
We have that
n^{\prime}_{j}+n^{\prime\prime}_{j}+1=n_{j}
.
Since
n_{j}\leq n=n^{\prime}+1
, then
n^{\prime}_{j},n^{\prime\prime}_{j}\leq n^{\prime}
.
Then, by the IH,
|I^{\prime}_{j}|\geq n^{\prime}_{j}+1
and
|I^{\prime\prime}_{j}|\geq n^{\prime\prime}_{j}+1
.
Therefore,
|I_{j}|=|I^{\prime}_{j}\cup I^{\prime\prime}_{j}|=|I^{\prime}_{j}|+|I^{\prime\prime}_{j}|\geq n^{\prime}_{j}+n^{\prime\prime}_{j}+1+1=n_{j}+1
.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle|I|\geq|{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\bigcup}_{1\leq j\leq m}I_{j}|={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\sum}_{1\leq j\leq m}|I_{j}|\geq{\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\sum}_{1\leq j\leq m}(n_{j}+1)={\mathchoice{\textstyle}{}{}{}\sum}_{1\leq j\leq m}n_{j}+m=n+m\geq n+1.

∎

Lemma 8

If
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}\vdash\emptyset
where for each
i\in I
,
\alpha_{i}\neq[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
for any
x
and
y
, then there are
j,k\in I
where
j\neq k
and
x=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{j})=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{k})
, and there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}
such that
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}
.

Proof.

Let
Q={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{(\alpha_{i})}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
Then
Q
is deterministic and, by
Lemma

4
,
Q\vdash\emptyset
.
Let
n
be the number of cuts in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
.
By
Lemma

7
,
|I|\geq n+1
.

Suppose, for contradiction, that for every
j,k\in I
where
j\neq k
, we have
\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{j})\neq\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{k})
.
Since
Q\vdash\emptyset
, for each
j\in I
,
\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{j})
must be bound by a cut, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
must contain
|I|
cuts.
This means
|I|=n
, contradicting the fact that
|I|\geq n+1
.
Therefore, there must be
j,k\in I
where
j\neq k
such that
\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{j})=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{k})
.

Hence, we can take
x=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{j})=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{k})
.
Since
P\vdash\emptyset
but
x
appears free in
\alpha_{j};P_{j}
and
\alpha_{k};P_{k}
, there must be a restriction on
x
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
containing the holes
{[\cdot]}_{j}
and
{[\cdot]}_{k}
.
We now obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}
from
P
by replacing the restriction on
x
in
P
with a hole, referring to the parallel component in which
\alpha_{j};P_{j}
appears as
P_{j}
and the component in which
\alpha_{k};P_{k}
appears as
P_{k}
.
Then, we obtain
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}
from
P_{j}
and
P_{k}
, respectively, by replacing
\alpha_{j};P_{j}
and
\alpha_{k};P_{k}
with a hole.
Clearly,
P={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{k};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}
.
∎

See
0.B.2

Proof.

By
Lemma

5
, there are S-context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}
with hole indices
I
and sequence of prefixed processes
{(\alpha_{i};P_{i})}_{i\in I}
such that
P\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
.
The next step depends on whether there is a forwarder process among the
\alpha_{i}
.

•

If there exists
j\in I
s.t.

for some

and

, then by

there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}},Q

{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}
.

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q)

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\llparenthesis}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}\mkern 1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\rrparenthesis}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}Q\{y/x\}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}=R^{\prime}

(by rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\text{Id}}]
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}

(by rules
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\nu}],[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{|}}],[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}}]
)

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R

(by rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\equiv}]
)

•

If for each
i\in I
,
\alpha_{i}\neq x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y
for any
x
and
y
, then by
Lemma

8
there are
j,k\in I
where
j\neq k
and
x=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{j})=\mathit{subj}(\alpha_{k})
for some
x
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{k};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}
.

We now show by cases on
\alpha_{j}
that there is
R^{\prime}
such that
(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{k};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}
.
First, note that by typability, if the type for
x
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
is
A
, then the type for
x
in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{k};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
is
\overline{A}
.
In the following cases, we determine more precisely the form of
A
by typing inversion on
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
, and then determine the form of
\alpha_{k}
by typing inversion using the form of
\overline{A}
.
Note that we can exclude any cases where
\alpha_{j}
or
\alpha_{k}
are forwarder processes, as we assume they are not.

–

If
\alpha_{j};P_{j}=\overline{x}[]
, then
A=\bm{1}
and
\overline{A}=\bot
.
Hence,
\alpha_{k};P_{k}=x();P_{k}
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\bm{1}\bot}]
, there is
R^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x();P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}.

–

If
\alpha_{j};P_{j}=\overline{x}[y];(P^{\prime}_{j}\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{j})
for some
y
, then
A=B\mathbin{\otimes}C
and
\overline{A}=\overline{B}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}}\overline{C}
for some
B
and
C
.
Hence,
\alpha_{k};P_{k}=x(z);P_{k}
for some
z
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{\otimes}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}}}]
, there is
R^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[y];(P^{\prime}_{j}\mathbin{|}P^{\prime\prime}_{j}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x(z);P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}.

–

If
\alpha_{j};P_{j}=\overline{x}.l^{\prime};P_{j}
, then
A={\oplus}\{l:B_{l}\}_{l\in L}
and
\overline{A}={{\binampersand}}\{l:\overline{B_{l}}\}_{l\in L}
for some
{(B_{l})}_{l\in L}
where
l^{\prime}\in L
.
Hence,
\alpha_{k};P_{k}=x.\mathtt{case}\{l:P_{k}^{l}\}_{l\in L}
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\oplus{\binampersand}}]
, there is
R^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.l^{\prime};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x.\mathtt{case}\{l:P_{k}^{l}\}_{l\in L}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}.

–

If
\alpha_{j};P_{j}=\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};P_{j}
, then
A={\oplus}B
and
\overline{A}={{\binampersand}}\overline{B}
for some
B
.
Hence,
\alpha_{k}={x.\mathtt{some}}_{w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}};P_{k}
for some
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathtt{some}}]
, there is
R^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{x.\mathtt{some}}_{w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}.

–

If
\alpha_{j};P_{j}=\overline{x}.\mathtt{none}
, then
A={\oplus}B
and
\overline{A}={{\binampersand}}\overline{B}
for some
B
.
Hence,
\alpha_{k}={x.\mathtt{some}}_{w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}};P_{k}
for some
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathtt{none}}]

R^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}.\mathtt{none}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{x.\mathtt{some}}_{w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}.

If
\alpha_{j};P_{j}={?}\overline{x}[y];P_{j}
for some

and
\overline{A}={!}\overline{B}
for some

[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{{?}{!}}]

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{?}\overline{x}[y];P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{!}x(z);P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime}.

–

Otherwise,
\alpha_{j}
is a receiving prefix and
\alpha_{k}
is thus a sending prefix.
By cases on
\alpha_{k}
, the proof is analogous to above.

In conclusion,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{j};P_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N_{k}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{k};P_{k}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}R^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}=R

(by rules
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\nu}],[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{|}}],[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}}]
)

\displaystyle P

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R.

(by rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\equiv}]

0.E.2
Lazy Semantics

Subject Reduction

Lemma 9

For both of the following items, assume
\Gamma\mathbin{\#}\Delta
.

1.

If
\forall i\in I.~\forall j\in J.~\mathit{bn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}})\mathbin{\#}\mathit{fn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}})
and
\forall i\in I.~{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[y_{i}];(P_{i}\mathbin{|}Q_{i}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A\mathbin{\otimes}B
and
\forall j\in J.~{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x(z);R_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta,x{:}\overline{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}}\overline{B}
, then
\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}(\bm{\nu}x)\Big(Q_{i}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}w)\Big(P_{i}\{w/y_{i}\}\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}R_{j}\{w/z\}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\Big)\Big){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}\vdash\Gamma,\Delta
.

2.

If
\forall i\in I.~\forall j\in J.~\mathit{bn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}})\mathbin{\#}\mathit{fn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}})
and
\forall i\in I.~{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{?}\overline{x}[y_{i}];P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}{?}A
and
\forall j\in J.~{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{!}x(z);Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Delta,x{:}{!}\overline{A}
, then
\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}(\bm{\nu}x)\Big((\bm{\nu}w)\Big(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P_{i}\{w/y_{i}\}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}Q_{j}\{w/z\}\Big)\mathbin{|}{!}x(z);Q_{j}\Big){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}\vdash\Gamma,\Delta
.

Proof.

Both items follow by induction on the structures of the D-contexts.
For each item, we detail the base case, where
\forall i\in I.~{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}=[\cdot]
and
\forall j\in J.~{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{D_{j}}}=[\cdot]
.
The inductive cases follow from the IH straightforwardly.

1.

\forall i\in I.~\frac{P_{i}\vdash\Gamma,y_{i}{:}A\quad Q_{i}\vdash\Delta,x{:}B}{\overline{x}[y_{i}];(P_{i}\mathbin{|}Q_{i})\vdash\Gamma,\Delta,x{:}A\mathbin{\otimes}B}\forall j\in J.~\frac{R_{j}\vdash\Lambda,z{:}\overline{A},x{:}\overline{B}}{x(z);R_{j}\vdash\Lambda,x{:}\overline{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle{{\binampersand}}$}}}\overline{B}}\implies\frac{\frac{\forall i\in I.~Q_{i}\vdash\Delta,x{:}B\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\forall i\in I.~P_{i}\{w/y_{i}\}\vdash\Gamma,w{:}A\hskip 7.3612pt\frac{\forall j\in J.~R_{j}\{w/z\}\vdash\Lambda,w{:}\overline{A},x{:}\overline{B}}{\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}R_{j}\{w/z\}\vdash\Lambda,w{:}\overline{A},x{:}\overline{B}}\hskip 7.3612pt\textsc{,fraction={===}}}{\forall i\in I.~(\bm{\nu}w)\Big(P_{i}\{w/y_{i}\}\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}R_{j}\{w/z\}\Big)\vdash\Gamma,\Lambda,x{:}\overline{B}}}{\forall i\in I.~(\bm{\nu}x)\Big(Q_{i}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}w)\Big(P_{i}\{w/y_{i}\}\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}R_{j}\{w/z\}\Big)\Big)\vdash\Gamma,\Delta,\Lambda}\quad\textsc{fraction={---}}}{\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}(\bm{\nu}x)\Big(Q_{i}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}w)\Big(P_{i}\{w/y_{i}\}\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}R_{j}\{w/z\}\Big)\Big)\vdash\Gamma,\Delta,\Lambda}\quad\textsc{,fraction={===}}

2.

This item depends on whether
x\in\mathit{fn}(P_{i})
or not, for each
i\in I
.
For simplicity, we only consider the cases where either
\forall i\in I.~x\in\mathit{fn}(P_{i})
or
\forall i\in I.~x\notin\mathit{fn}(P_{i})
.

Theorem 0.E.3

(SR for the Lazy Semantics)

If
P\vdash\Gamma
and
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}Q
, then
Q\vdash\Gamma
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of the reduction.

•

Rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\rightsquigarrow}_{\text{Id}}]

Definition 15

(Multi-hole Non-deterministic Reduction Contexts)
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}::=[\cdot]\mathbin{\scalebox{1.3}{$\mid$}}(\bm{\nu}x)(P\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})\mathbin{\scalebox{1.3}{$\mid$}}P\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}\mathbin{\scalebox{1.3}{$\mid$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}

Lemma 10

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha;P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A
and
x=\mathit{subj}(\alpha)
, then there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and
{(\alpha_{i};P_{i})}_{i\in I}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha;P{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
where
x\notin\mathit{fn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})
and
x\in\bigcap_{i\in I}\mathit{fn}(\alpha_{i};P_{i})
and there is
i^{\prime}\in I
such that
\alpha_{i^{\prime}};P_{i^{\prime}}=\alpha;P
.

Lemma 11

For every multi-hole ND-context
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
with indices
I
:

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
has two or more holes, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}
with indices
I_{1}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}
with indices
I_{2}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
where
I_{1}\mathbin{\#}I_{2}
and
I=I_{1}\cup I_{2}
.

•

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
has only one hole, there is
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}
.

Lemma 13

If

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})[\beta_{j};Q_{j}]_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}R,

then

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{j};Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}R.

Proof.

By induction on the structures of
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}
.
By
Lemma

11
, we only have to consider two cases for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
(
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}
), and similarly for
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}
.
We only detail the base case (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}
) and a representative inductive case (
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}
).

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}
have only one hole; w.l.o.g., assume
I=J=\{1\}
.

\displaystyle\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{1};P_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}})[\alpha_{1};P_{1}]={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{1};P_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{1};P_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}

\displaystyle\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})[\beta_{1};Q_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}})[\beta_{1};Q_{1}]={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};Q_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};Q_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}

The thesis follows by assumption and equality.

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}
.
Note that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}
has only one hole; w.l.o.g., assume
J=\{1\}
.

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})[\beta_{1};Q_{1}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}})[\beta_{1};Q_{1}]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}))[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};Q_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\Big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};Q_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{1}}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};Q_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};Q_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]})

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}R

(by rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\rightsquigarrow}_{\nu\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}}]
on (
11
))

∎

Theorem 0.E.4

If
P\vdash\Gamma
and
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R
, then
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}R
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of the reduction.
The inductive cases of rules
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\equiv}]
,
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\nu}]
,
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{|}}]
, and
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}}]
follow from the IH straightforwardly, using the corresponding closure rule for
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}
.
As representative base case, we consider rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\bm{1}\bot}]
:
P=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x();Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R
.

By inversion of typing,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A
, so by
Lemma

10
, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and
{(\alpha_{i};P_{i})}_{i\in I}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
where
x\notin\mathit{fn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})
and
x\in\bigcap_{i\in I}\mathit{fn}(\alpha_{i};P_{i})
and there is
i^{\prime}\in I
such that
\alpha_{i^{\prime}};P_{i^{\prime}}=\overline{x}[]
.
Similarly, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}
and
{(\beta_{j};Q_{j})}_{j\in J}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x();Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{j};Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{j\in J}
where
x\notin\mathit{fn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})
and
x\in\bigcap_{j\in J}\mathit{fn}(\beta_{j};Q_{j})
and there is
j^{\prime}\in J
such that
\beta_{j^{\prime}};Q_{j^{\prime}}=x();Q
.

By
Lemma

12
,
\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}=\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and
\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})[\beta_{j};Q_{j}]_{j\in J}=\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C^{\prime}_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{j};Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
By typability, there is
I^{\prime}\subseteq I
such that
\forall i\in I^{\prime}.~\alpha_{i}\bowtie\overline{x}[]
and
\forall i\in I\setminus I^{\prime}.~\alpha_{i}\not\bowtie\overline{x}[]
; hence,
i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}
.
Similarly, there is
J^{\prime}\subseteq J
such that
\forall j\in J^{\prime}.~\beta_{j}\bowtie x()
and
\forall j\in J\setminus J^{\prime}.~\beta_{j}\not\bowtie x()
; hence,
j^{\prime}\in J^{\prime}
.
Then, by
Definition

3
,
\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}\mathrel{\succeq_{x}}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and
\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})[\beta_{j};Q_{j}]_{j\in J}\mathrel{\succeq_{x}}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x();Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\rightsquigarrow}_{\bm{1}\bot}]
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\overline{x}[]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{j\in J^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{j}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}x();Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]})

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{x}R,

so by rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathrel{\succeq_{x}}}]
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)(\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}\mathbin{|}\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}})[\beta_{j};Q_{j}]_{j\in J})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{x}R.

Then, by
Lemma

13
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{j};Q_{j}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{j\in J})\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{x}R.

As second base case, we consider rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\shortrightarrow}_{\text{Id}}]
:
P=(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R
.

By inversion of typing,
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\vdash\Gamma,x{:}A,y{:}\overline{A}
, so by
Lemma

10
, there are
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and
{(\alpha_{i};P_{i})}_{i\in I}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}={\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}
where
x\notin\mathit{fn}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})
and
x\in\bigcap_{i\in I}\mathit{fn}(\alpha_{i};P_{i})
and there is
i^{\prime}\in I
such that
\alpha_{i^{\prime}};P_{i^{\prime}}=[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
.

By
Lemma

12
,
\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}=\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
By typability, there is
I^{\prime}\subseteq I
such that
\forall i\in I^{\prime}.~\alpha_{i}=[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
and
\forall i\in I\setminus I^{\prime}.~\alpha_{i}\not=[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]
; hence,
i^{\prime}\in I^{\prime}
.

Then, by
Definition

3
,
\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}\mathrel{\succeq_{x,y}}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
By rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\rightsquigarrow}_{\text{Id}}]
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)\Big(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{C_{i}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}y]{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}\mathbin{|}Q\Big)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{x,y}R,

so by rule
[{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\rightsquigarrow}_{\mathrel{\succeq_{x,y}}}]
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)(\mathit{flat}({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}})[\alpha_{i};P_{i}]_{i\in I}\mathbin{|}Q)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{x,y}R.

Then, by
Lemma

13
,

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{i};P_{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}_{i\in I}\mathbin{|}Q)\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{x,y}R.

∎

Theorem 0.E.5

(DF: Lazy Semantics)

If
P\vdash\emptyset
and

, then
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}R
for some
S
and
R
.

Proof.

As a corollary of
Theorems

0.B.2
and
0.E.4
.
∎

Appendix 0.F
Proofs of Subject Reduction and Subject Expansion for
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}

Here we prove
Theorems

3.1
and
3.2
(subject reduction and subject expansion, respectively) for
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
.

0.F.1
Subject Reduction

Lemma 14

(Substitution Lemma for
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
)

1.

(Linear) If
\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma\vDash M:\tau
,
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
, and
\Theta;\Delta\vDash N:\sigma

then

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\vDash M\{N/{x}\}:\tau
.

2.

(Unrestricted) If
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
,
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
,
\eta_{i}=\sigma
, and
\Theta;\cdot\vDash N:\sigma

then

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash M\{N/{x}[i]\}
.

Proof.

1.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
.
There are six cases to be analyzed:

(a)

M={x}

In this case,
\Theta;{x}:\sigma\vDash{x}:\sigma
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
{x}\{N/{x}\}=N
, since
\Delta\vDash N:\sigma
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

(b)

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
, and the derivation is the following:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{x}:\sigma\vDash M^{\prime}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vDash B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},{x}:\sigma\vDash M^{\prime}B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
, and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different. Since
\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
, by IH, the result holds for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Gamma_{1},\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

which gives the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vDash B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\vDash(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\})B:\tau

From
Figure

13
,
(M^{\prime}B)\{N/{x}\}=(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\})B
, and the result follows.

(c)

M=M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
, for
y\neq x
. Therefore,

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,{x}:\sigma\vDash M^{\prime}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k},{x}:\sigma\vDash M^{\prime}[{y}_{1},\dots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}
.
By IH, the result follows for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}:\tau

and we have the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}shar]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k},\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

From
Figure

13
,
M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]\{N/{x}\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
,
and the result follows.

(d)

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}
with
x\not=y
,

\Theta;\Gamma,{x}:\sigma\vDash M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega,{x}:\sigma\vDash M[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

and
M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]\{N/{x}\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}[\leftarrow{y}]
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta;\Gamma,\Delta\vDash M\{N/{x}\}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega,\Delta\vDash M\{N/{x}\}[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

(e)

If
M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x\not=y_{1},\dots,y_{k}
,

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},y_{1}:\delta,\dots,y_{k}:\delta,x:\sigma\vDash M^{\prime}:\tau

\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vDash C:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\vDash M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

and
M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}\{N/x\}=M^{\prime}\{N/x\}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
. Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Delta,y_{1}:\delta,\dots,y_{k}:\delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/x\}:\tau

\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vDash C:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/x\}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

(f)

If
M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil
then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, and the proofs is similar to the case above.

2.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
.
There are three cases to be analyzed:

(a)

M={x}[i]
.

In this case,

\mathtt{[FS{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;{x}:\eta_{i}\vDash{x}:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}var^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\cdot\vDash{x}[i]:\sigma

and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
{x}[i]\{N/{x}[i]\}=N
, since
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash M\{N/{x}[i]\}
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

(b)

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
, and one has the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\vDash M:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau\quad\Theta,x^{!}:\sigma;\Gamma_{2}\vDash B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\sim\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vDash M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different.

By the induction hypothesis, we get
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x[i]}\}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau
, which gives the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{2}\vDash B:(\delta^{k},\epsilon^{\prime})

\epsilon\sim\epsilon^{\prime}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vDash(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\})B:\tau

From
Figure

13
,
M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle\{N/{x}[i]\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
, and the result follows.

(c)

M=M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
, for
y\neq x
. Therefore,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vDash M^{\prime}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}shar]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\vDash M^{\prime}[{y}_{1},\dots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow y]:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}
.
By the induction hypothesis, the result follows for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau

and we have the derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}shar]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

From
Figure

13

M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]\{N/{x}[i]\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
,
and the result follows.

(d)

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
with
x\not=y
,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega\vDash M[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

and
M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]\{N/{x}[i]\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}[\leftarrow{y}]
.
By the induction hypothesis:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash M\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega\vDash M\{N/{x}[i]\}[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

(e)

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
with
x\not=y
,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,y_{1}:\delta,\dots,y_{k}:\delta\vDash M:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Delta\vDash C:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,\Delta\vDash M{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

and
M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}\{N/{x}[i]\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
.
By the induction hypothesis:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,y_{1}:\delta,\dots,y_{k}:\delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Delta\vDash C:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma,\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

(f)

M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/y^{!}\rrceil
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\llfloor U/y^{!}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={x}[i]
,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta,{y}:\epsilon_{1};\Gamma\vDash M^{\prime}:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon_{2}

\epsilon_{1}\sim\epsilon_{2}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M^{\prime}\llfloor U/y^{!}\rrceil:\tau

and
M^{\prime}\llfloor U/y^{!}\rrceil\{N/{x}[i]\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/y^{!}\rrceil
.
Then by the induction hypothesis:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta,{y}:\epsilon_{1};\Gamma\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon_{2}

\epsilon_{1}\sim\epsilon_{2}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/y^{!}\rrceil:\tau

∎

Theorem 0.F.1

(SR in
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
)

If
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
and
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}
then
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M^{\prime}:\tau
.

Proof.

By structural induction on the reduction rule from Fig.
13
applied in
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}
.

1.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
.

Then
M=(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B
and the reduction is:

\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}

(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

where
M^{\prime}=N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,\dots,{x}_{j}:\sigma\vDash N:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\vDash N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B:\tau

for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
x\notin\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.
Notice that:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,\dots,{x}_{j}:\sigma\vDash N:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\vDash N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Therefore
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash M^{\prime}:\tau
and the result follows.

2.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}
.

Then
M=N[{x}_{1},\dots,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdot\lbag N_{k}\rbag
.
The reduction is:

C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\dots\lbag N_{k}\rbag\qquad N\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

N[{x}_{1},\!\dots\!,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\dots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil

and
M^{\prime}=N{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\dots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
.
To simplify the proof we take
k=2
, as the case
k>2
is similar.
Therefore
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\lbag N_{2}\rbag
.

Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
we get a derivation: (we omit the labels
\mathtt{[FS:Esub]}
and
\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}
)

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,{x}_{2}:\sigma\vDash N:\tau\hskip 9.24994pt{x}\notin\mathit{dom}(\Gamma)\hskip 9.24994ptk\not=0

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{2}\vDash N[{x}_{1},{x}_{2}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash C\star U:(\sigma^{2},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N[{x}_{1},{x}_{2}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. Consider the wf derivation for
\Pi_{1,2}
: (we omit the labels
\mathtt{[FS:Esub^{!}]}
and
\mathtt{[FS:Esub^{\ell}]}
)

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,{x}_{2}:\sigma\vDash N:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash C:\sigma^{k}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N{\langle\!|}C/{x}_{1},{x}_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon

\eta\sim\epsilon

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N{\langle\!|}C/{x}_{1},{x}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

and the result follows.

3.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}
.

Then
M=N{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}
where
\mathsf{head}(N)={x}_{j}
.
The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(N)={x}_{j}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}_{{i}}]}

N{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}

and
M^{\prime}=N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}
.
Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\widetilde{x}:\sigma^{k-1},x_{j}:\sigma\vDash N:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash C:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
\Delta=\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
with
\Theta;\Delta_{i}\vDash C_{i}:\sigma
.
By
Lemma

14
, we obtain the derivation
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}:\tau
via:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta_{i},\widetilde{x}:\sigma^{k-1}\vDash N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}:\tau

\Theta;\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\vDash C\setminus C_{i}:\sigma^{k-1}

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

4.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}
.

Then
M=N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
. The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(N)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\lbag N_{i}\rbag^{!}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}

N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil

and
M^{\prime}=N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash N:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

By
Lemma

14
, we obtain the derivation
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}
, and the result follows from:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

5.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}
.

Then
M=C[N]
and the reduction is as follows:

N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N^{\prime}

\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}

C[N]\mathbin{\longrightarrow}C[N^{\prime}]

with
M^{\prime}=C[N^{\prime}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
C
.
There are three cases:

(a)

C=[\cdot]\ B
.

In this case
M=N\ B
, for some
B
.
Since
\Gamma\vdash M:\tau
one has a derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash N:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
.
From
\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash N:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
and the reduction
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N^{\prime}
, one has by IH that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash N^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
.
Finally, we may type the following:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash N^{\prime}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash B:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N^{\prime}\ B:\tau

Since
M^{\prime}=(C[N^{\prime}])=N^{\prime}B
, the result follows.

(b)

Cases
C=[\cdot]{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
and
C=[\cdot][\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
are similar to the previous.

6.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{\ell}]}
.

Then
M=N[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
k\neq\mathsf{size}(C)
,
\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{lfv}(N)\setminus\{\widetilde{x}\})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)
and the reduction is:

k\neq\mathsf{size}(C)

\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{lfv}(N)\setminus\{\widetilde{x}\})\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{\ell}]}

N[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\mathbin{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

where
M^{\prime}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
.
Since
\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\vDash M
, one has a derivation:

\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\vDash N:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub]}

\Theta,x:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k}\vDash N[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash C\star U:(\sigma^{j},\epsilon)

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash N[x_{1},\dots,x_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
.
We may type the following:

\mathit{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta)=\widetilde{x}

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

since
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
contain assignments on the free variables in
M
and
B
.
Therefore,
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau
, by applying
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
as required.

7.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}
.

Then
M=N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
and
U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}
and the reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(N)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\mathtt{1}^{!}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}

N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N\{\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil

with
M^{\prime}=N\{\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
.
By the induction hypothesis, one has the derivation:

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash N:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

By
Lemma

14
, there exists a derivation
\Pi_{1}
of
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash N\{\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]\}:\tau
.
Thus,

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vDash N\{\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]\}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon

\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash N\{\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

8.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
M=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B
and the reduction is:

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(C)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U\mathbin{\longrightarrow}\displaystyle\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}

and
M^{\prime}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}
.
Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau
, one has the derivation:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vDash\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vDash C:\sigma^{k}

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash U:\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Delta\vDash C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\epsilon)\quad\eta\sim\epsilon

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vDash\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:\ C\star U:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}:\tau

9.

The proof for the cases of
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}
,
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{4}]}
proceed similarly.
∎

0.F.2
Subject Expansion

.
