Figure 18:
Well-Typed Rules for
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
.

Lemma 15

(Anti-Substitution Lemma for
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
)

1.

(Linear)
If
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M\{N/{x}\}:\tau
then there exists
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
\sigma
such that
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma\vdash M:\tau
and
\Theta;\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
.

2.

(Unrestricted)
If
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash M\{N/{x}[i]\}
with
\eta_{i}=\sigma
then
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash M:\tau
and
\Theta;\cdot\vdash N:\sigma
.

Proof.

1.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}
.
There are six cases to be analyzed:

(a)

M={x}
.

(b)

In this case,
{x}\{N/{x}\}=N
and so
\Gamma\vdash N:\sigma
. Take
\Gamma^{\prime}=\text{-}
and
\Delta=\Gamma
then
\Theta;{x}:\sigma\vdash{x}:\sigma
and
\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
by hypothesis, the result follows.

(c)

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

From
Figure

13
,
(M^{\prime}B)\{N/{x}\}=(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\})B
. Let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
for some
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
and the derivation is the following:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vdash B:(\delta^{j},\eta)

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\})B:\tau

where
j\geq 0
. By IH there exists
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Delta,\sigma
such that
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Delta
with
\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
and
\Theta;\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
. Which gives the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:(\delta^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vdash B:(\delta^{j},\eta)

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}B:\tau

By taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
the result follows.

(d)

M=M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
with
y\neq x
.

Then from
Figure

13
,
M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]\{N/{x}\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
. Let
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}
with
k\not=0
. Therefore,

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[TS{:}shar]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

By IH there exists
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Delta,\sigma
such that
\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,\Delta
with
\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
and
\Theta;\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
. Which gives the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k},{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}[{y}_{1},\dots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

By taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}
the result follows.

(e)

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]
with
x\not=y
.

Then
M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]\{N/{x}\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}\}[\leftarrow{y}]
. Therefore,

\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M\{N/{x}\}:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma,{y}:\omega\vdash M\{N/{x}\}[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

By IH there exists
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta,\sigma
such that
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
with
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
and
\Theta;\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
. Which gives the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}weak]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\omega,{x}:\sigma\vdash M[\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

By taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\omega
the result follows.

(f)

If
M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
with
x\not=y_{1},\dots,y_{k}
.

Then
M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}\{N/x\}=M^{\prime}\{N/x\}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
and

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},y_{1}:\delta,\dots,y_{k}:\delta\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/x\}:\tau

\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vdash C:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/x\}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

By IH there exists
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Delta,\sigma
such that
\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,\Delta
with
\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta,{x}:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
and
\Theta;\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
. Which gives the derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\delta,\dots,y_{k}:\delta,x:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau

\quad\Theta;\Gamma_{2}\vdash C:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\vdash M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

By taking
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
the result follows.

(g)

If
M=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil
then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\llfloor U/{y}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, and the proofs is similar to the case above.

2.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
.
There are three cases to be analyzed:

(a)

M={x}[i]
.

Observe that
{x}[i]\{N/{x}[i]\}=N
and let
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash{x}[i]\{N/{x}[i]\}:\sigma
with
\eta_{i}=\sigma

In this case
\Gamma=\text{-}
, and we have both

\mathtt{[TS{:}var^{\ell}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;{x}:\eta_{i}\vdash{x}:\sigma

\mathtt{[TS{:}var^{!}]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\cdot\vdash{x}[i]:\sigma

and
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash N:\sigma
and the result follows.

(b)

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

From
Figure

13
,
M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle\{N/{x}[i]\}=M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
, let
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\})B:\tau
where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
and
\eta_{i}=\sigma
we derrive:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{2}\vdash B:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash(M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\})B:\tau

for some strict type
\delta
. By IH
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\vdash M^{\prime}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau
and
\Theta;\cdot\vdash N:\sigma
and we derrive:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1}\vdash M^{\prime}:(\delta^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau\quad\Theta,x^{!}:\sigma;\Gamma_{2}\vdash B:(\delta^{j},\epsilon^{\prime})

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash M^{\prime}\ B:\tau

and the result follows.

(c)

M=M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]
.

From
Figure

13

, let

with

and

, then we derive:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[TS{:}shar]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash M^{\prime}\{N/{x}[i]\}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]:\tau

By IH
\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
and

and we derrive:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}_{1}:\delta,\dots,{y}_{k}:\delta\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau\quad{y}\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[TS{:}shar]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash M^{\prime}[{y}_{1},\dots,{y}_{k}\leftarrow y]:\tau

and the result follows.

(d)

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow{y}]

From

, let

with

and

, then we derive:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash M\{N/{x}[i]\}:\tau

and the result follows.

(e)

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/y_{1},\dots,y_{k}{|\!\rangle}
.

Theorem 0.F.2

(Subject Expansion in
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
)

If
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
and
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}
then
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M:\tau
.

Proof.

By structural induction on the reduction rule from Fig.
13
applied in
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}
.

1.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
.

Then
M^{\prime}=N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is:

\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}

(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

where
M=(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,\dots,{x}_{j}:\sigma\vdash N:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\vdash N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash B:(\sigma^{j},\eta)

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. Notice that:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,\dots,{x}_{j}:\sigma\vdash N:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\vdash N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash B:(\sigma^{j},\eta)

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B:\tau

Therefore
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B:\tau
and the result follows.

Then
M=(\lambda x.N[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])B
and the reduction is:

2.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}.

Then
M^{\prime}=N{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\dots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
where
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\dots\cdot\lbag N_{k}\rbag
.
The reduction is:

C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\dots\lbag N_{k}\rbag\qquad N\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

N[{x}_{1},\!\dots\!,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\dots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil

and
M=N[{x}_{1},\!\dots\!,{x}_{k}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.
To simplify the proof we take
k=2
, as the case
k>2
is similar.
Therefore
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\lbag N_{2}\rbag
.

Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
we get a derivation(we omit the labels
\mathtt{[TS:Esub^{!}]}
and
\mathtt{[TS:Esub^{\ell}]}
):

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,{x}_{2}:\sigma\vdash N:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash C:\sigma^{2}

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N{\langle\!|}C/{x}_{1},{x}_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vdash U:\eta

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N{\langle\!|}C/{x}_{1},{x}_{2}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. Consider the typing derivation:(we omit the labels
\mathtt{[TS:Esub]}
and
\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}
)

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}_{1}:\sigma,{x}_{2}:\sigma\vdash N:\tau

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{2}\vdash N[{x}_{1},{x}_{2}\leftarrow{x}]:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash C\star U:(\sigma^{2},\eta)

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N[{x}_{1},{x}_{2}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

and the result follows.

3.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}
.

Then
M^{\prime}=N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}
where
\mathsf{head}(N)={x}_{j}
.
The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(N)={x}_{j}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}_{{i}}]}

N{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}

and
M=N{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}
.
Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta_{i},\widetilde{x}:\sigma^{k-1}\vdash N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}:\tau

\Theta;\Delta^{\prime}_{i}\vdash C\setminus C_{i}:\sigma^{k-1}

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N\{C_{i}/x_{j}\}{\langle\!|}(C\setminus C_{i})/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
\Delta=\Delta_{i},\Delta_{i}^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

15
, we obtain the derivation
\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\widetilde{x}:\sigma^{k-1},x_{j}:\sigma\vdash N:\tau
and
\Theta;\Delta_{i}\vdash C_{i}:\sigma
via:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\widetilde{x}:\sigma^{k-1},x_{j}:\sigma\vdash N:\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash C:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub^{\ell}]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

4.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}
.

Then
M^{\prime}=N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
where
\mathsf{head}(M)={x}[i]
. The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(N)={x}[i]

U_{i}=\lbag N_{i}\rbag^{!}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}

N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil

and
M=N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
. Since
\Theta;\Gamma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
we get the following derivation:

\Theta,x^{!}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vdash U:\eta

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vdash N\{N_{i}/{x}[i]\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

By
Lemma

15
, we obtain the derivation
\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash N:\tau
, and the result follows from:

\Theta,{x}:\eta;\Gamma\vdash N:\tau

\Theta;\text{-}\vdash U:\eta

\mathtt{[TS{:}Esub^{!}]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vdash N\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil:\tau

5.

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}
.

Then
M^{\prime}=C[N^{\prime}]
and the reduction is as follows:

N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N^{\prime}

\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}

C[N]\mathbin{\longrightarrow}C[N^{\prime}]

with
M=C[N]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
C
.
There are three cases:

(a)

C=[\cdot]\ B
.

In this case
M^{\prime}=N^{\prime}\ B
, for some
B
.
Since
\Gamma\vdash M^{\prime}:\tau
one has a derivation:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash N^{\prime}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash B:(\sigma^{j},\eta)

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N^{\prime}\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
.
From
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash N^{\prime}:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
and the reduction
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}N^{\prime}
, one has by IH that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash N:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau
.
Finally, we may type the following:

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash N:(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau

\Theta;\Delta\vdash B:(\sigma^{j},\eta)

\mathtt{[TS{:}app]}

\Theta;\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\vdash N\ B:\tau

Since
M=(C[N])=NB
, the result follows.

(b)

Cases
C=[\cdot]{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
and
C=[\cdot][\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
are similar to the previous.

6.

Rules
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{\ell}]}
,
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail^{!}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}
.
These cases are trivial, since
\mathtt{1}
,
\mathtt{1}^{!}
and
\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}
are not well-typed.
∎

Appendix 0.G
Proof of Tight Correctness of the Translation under the Lazy Semantics

Here we prove
Theorem

4.2
.

Definition 17

(Success)
.

We define define
\mathsf{head}({\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\lambda})={\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\lambda}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\lambda}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}={\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\pi}
.
We define
success
for terms
M\in\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
and
P\in{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
.

•

{{M}}\Downarrow{{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\lambda}}
if and only if, there exist
M^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,M_{k}^{\prime}\in\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
such that
{M}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{*}M^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})={\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\lambda}
.

•

{P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\Downarrow}}{{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\pi}}
if and only if, there exist
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
such that
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}(Q_{1}\mathbin{|}{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\pi})\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q_{2}
.

•

{P}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\Downarrow}}{{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\pi}}
if and only if, there exist
S
and
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
such that
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}_{S}^{*}(Q_{1}\mathbin{|}{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\pi})\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q_{2}
.

0.G.1
Type Preservation

Lemma 16

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma^{j}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\tau_{1},m)}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\tau_{2},n)}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{j},\eta){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\tau_{1},m)}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{k},\eta){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\tau_{2},n)}
hold, provided that
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},n
and
m
are as follows:

1.

If
j>k
then take
\tau_{1}
to be an arbitrary type,
m=0
, take
\tau_{2}
to be
\sigma
and
n=j-k
.

2.

If
j<k
then take
\tau_{1}
to be
\sigma
,
m=k-j
, take
\tau_{2}
to be an arbitrary type and
n=0
.

3.

Otherwise, if
j=k
then take
m=n=0
. In this case,
\tau_{1}.\tau_{2}
are unimportant.

Proof.

This proof proceeds by analyzing the conditions on types, following Paulus et al.
[
21
]
.
∎

Lemma 17

If

\eta\sim\epsilon
then the following hold:

1.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},x^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}}

then

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},x^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\epsilon{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}}
.

2.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

then

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{j},\epsilon)\rightarrow\tau{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}
.

Proof.

The proof is by mutual induction on the the derivations of If
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},x^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{j},\eta)\rightarrow\tau{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}
and on the structure of

We use Def.

that establishes
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}=!{\binampersand}_{\eta_{i}\in\eta}\{i:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}\}
and by duality

.

we use the argument that the typing of

.

•

y\not=x

Then we have that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},x^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\epsilon{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}}
By the IH there exists a derivation
\Pi_{1}^{\prime}
of
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{y}}\leftarrow{y}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vdash u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},y^{\ell}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\sigma,i)}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},y^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}},x^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\epsilon{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}}
and then redo the steps above and obtain
\overline{u}.\mathtt{some};u(y);\overline{y}.\mathtt{some};P\vdash u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{k},\eta)\rightarrow\tau{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},x^{!}:\overline{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\epsilon{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}}
.

4.

The analysis of the other cases proceeds similarly. ∎

Theorem 0.G.1

(Type Preservation)

Let
B
and
{M}
be a bag and an term in
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
, respectively.

1.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash B:(\sigma^{k},\eta)

then

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}B{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vDash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{k},\eta){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\sigma,i)},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}
.

2.

If
\Theta;\Gamma\vDash M:\tau

then

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\vDash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},u:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}
.

Proof.

The proof is by mutual induction on the typing derivation of
B
and
{M^{\prime}}
, with an analysis for the last rule applied.
Recall that the translation of types (
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}-{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}
) has been given in
Def.
12
. We will be silently performing
[
Tcontract
]
to split the translation of unrestricted context in derivation trees of
{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
processes as well as combining multiple
[
Tweaken
]
when convenient.

1.

For
B=C\star U
:

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash C:\sigma^{k}

\Theta;\cdot\vDash U:\eta

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}

\Theta;\Gamma\vDash C\star U:(\sigma^{k},\eta)

Our translation gives:

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}={x.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{x}[x^{\ell}].({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[x^{!}].({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[]))
.
In addition, the translation of
(\sigma^{k},\eta)
is:

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\sigma^{k},\eta){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\sigma,i)}=\oplus(({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\sigma,i)})\otimes(({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}})\otimes(\mathbf{1})))\quad\text{(for some $i\geq 0$ and strict type $\sigma$)}

And one can build the following type derivation (rules from Fig.
3
):

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}}\vdash{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},x^{\ell}:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{(\sigma,i)},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\vdash x_{i}:{\binampersand}_{\eta_{i}\in\eta}\{i;{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}\},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

[
T
!
]

{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\vdash x^{!}:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

[
T
\bm{1}
]

\overline{x}[]\vdash x:\mathbf{1}

\overline{x}[]\vdash x:\mathbf{1},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

[
T
\mathbin{\otimes}
]

\overline{x}[x^{!}].({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[])\vdash x:({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\eta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}})\otimes(\mathbf{1}),{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

[
T
\mathbin{\otimes}
]

(c)

For

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\text{-}\vDash\mathtt{1}^{!}:\sigma_{1}

\Theta;\cdot\vDash M^{\prime}:\sigma_{2}

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\cdot\vDash\lbag M^{\prime}\rbag^{!}:\sigma_{2}

\mathtt{[FS{:}\diamond-bag^{!}]}

\Theta;\cdot\vDash\mathtt{1}^{!}\diamond\lbag M^{\prime}\rbag^{!}:\sigma_{1}\diamond\sigma_{2}

By the induction hypothesis we have that
\Theta;\cdot\vDash M^{\prime}:\sigma
implies
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\vDash x_{i}:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}
. Thus,

[
T
{{\binampersand}}\mathtt{none}
]

\overline{x_{i}}.\mathtt{none}\vdash x_{i}:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

[
Tweaken
]

\overline{x_{i}}.\mathtt{none}\vdash x_{i}:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\vdash x_{i}:{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\sigma_{2}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}},{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\Theta{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}

{\binampersand}

0.G.2
Completeness

Definition 18

(Linearly Partially Open Terms)
.

We say that a
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
-term
M
is
linearly partially open
if
\forall x\in\mathsf{lfv}(M)
implies that
x
is not a sharing variable.

Proposition 3

Suppose
N
is a well-formed linearly partially open
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
-term with
\mathsf{head}(N)=x
(
x
denoting either linear or unrestricted occurrence of
x
) .
Then,

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}y_{1})(\cdots(\bm{\nu}y_{m})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}x{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{n}\mathbin{|}P_{m})\cdots\mathbin{|}P_{1})

which we shall denote as:

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{y})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}x{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{n}\mathbin{|}P)
,
for some names
\widetilde{y}=
and
n
, and processes
P
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the structure of
N
.

1.

N={x}
: then
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{x}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};[x\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]
. Hence
P={\bf 0}
and
\widetilde{y}=\emptyset
.

2.

N={x}[j]
: then
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{x}[j]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}={?}\overline{x^{!}}[x_{i}];\overline{{x}_{i}}.j;[x_{i}\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]
. Hence
P={\bf 0}
and
\widetilde{y}=\emptyset
.

3.

N=M\ (C\star U)
: then
\mathsf{head}(M\ (C\star U))=\mathsf{head}(M)=x
and

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M\ (C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x].([v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}))

The result follows by induction hypothesis applied on
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
.

4.

N=(M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
: this case does not apply since
\mathsf{head}(N)\neq x
.

5.

N=M{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}
: then
\mathsf{head}(M{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M)=x
. Let
C=\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdot\lbag M_{k}\rbag

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

The result follows by induction hypothesis applied on
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
.

6.

N=M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
: then
\mathsf{head}(M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil)=\mathsf{head}(M)=x
and

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}~{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}})

The result follows by induction hypothesis applied on
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
.

∎

Theorem 0.G.2

(Completeness (Under
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}
))

If
{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}{M}
for a well-formed closed
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
-term
N
, then
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{\ast}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
.

Proof.

By induction on the reduction rule applied to infer
{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}{M}
.
We have five cases.

1.

Case
\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}
:

Then
N=(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]))B\mathbin{\longrightarrow}(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=M
, where
B=C\star U
.
The result follows from

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)(\overline{v}.\mathtt{some};v(x);\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}v)(v(x);\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

2.

: Then

3.

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{\ell}]}
:

Then we have

N=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}
with

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\cdots,x_{k},x_{j}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

(13)

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}(\bm{\nu}y)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{x}_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{y}\mathbin{|}P)\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})

\displaystyle\cdots)\qquad(*)

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}

Let us consider the case when

and

the other cases proceed similarly. Then we have the following reduction:

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

) and taking

4.

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fetch^{!}]}
:

Then,

N=M^{\prime}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
with
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x^{!}[k]
,
U_{k}=\lbag N\rbag^{!}
and
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}\{N/x^{!}\}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil=M

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}*(\bm{\nu}x^{!})((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{y})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{j}\mathbin{|}P)\mathbin{|}~{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x^{!})((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{y})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}^{!}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{j}\mathbin{|}P)\mathbin{|}~{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x^{!})((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{y})(\overline{j}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}P)\mathbin{|}~{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}})={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

8.

Case
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{1}]}
:
Then we have

N=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U
and
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}=M
where
\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{lfv}(C)
. Also,

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)(\overline{v}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\widetilde{x}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}\overline{\widetilde{x}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{u}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\widetilde{y}}.\mathtt{none}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

9.

Cases
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{2}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS:Cons_{3}]}
: These cases follow by IH similarly to Case 7.

10.

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{4}]}
:
Then we have

N=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil
and
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}=M
, and

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}~{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}})

(17)

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x^{!})(\overline{u}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\widetilde{x}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}~{!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}})\equiv\overline{u}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\widetilde{x}}.\mathtt{none}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

∎

0.G.3
Soundness

Theorem 0.G.3

(Weak Soundness (Under
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}
))

If
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{*}Q
for a well-formed closed
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
-term
N
, then there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
N^{\prime}
such that
Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{*}Q^{\prime}
,
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{*}N^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\equiv Q^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
N
and then induction on the number of reductions of
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{*}Q
.

1.

Base case:

N={x}
,
N={x}[j]
,
N=\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}
and
N=\lambda x.(M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])
.
.

No reductions can take place, and the result follows trivially.

Q={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{0}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
and
N\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{0}N=N^{\prime}
.

2.

N=M(C\star U)
.

Then,

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))
, and we are able to perform the reductions from
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
.

We now proceed by induction on
k
, with
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{k}Q
. There are two main cases:

(a)

When
k=0
the thesis follows easily:

We have

Q={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{0}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
and
M(C\star U)\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{0}M(C\star U)=N^{\prime}
.

(b)

The interesting case is when
k\geq 1
.

Then, for some process
R
and
n,m
such that
k=n+m
, we have the following:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{m}(\bm{\nu}v)(R\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u])))\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{n}Q

Thus, the first
m\geq 0
reduction steps are internal to
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}
; type preservation in
{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
ensures that, if they occur, these reductions do not discard the possibility of synchronizing with
\overline{v}.\mathtt{some}
. Then, the first of the
n\geq 0
reduction steps towards
Q
is a synchronization between
R
and
{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)}
.

We consider two sub-cases, depending on the values of
m
and
n
:

i.

m=0
and
n\geq 1
:

Then
R={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}
as
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{0}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}
.
Notice that there are two possibilities of having an unguarded:

A.

M=(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil\quad(p,q\geq 0)

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z^{!}_{q})(\cdots(\bm{\nu}z^{!}_{1})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}~{!}z^{!}_{1}(z_{1});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{z_{1}})

\displaystyle\cdots\mathbin{|}~{!}z^{!}_{q}(z_{q});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{q}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{z_{q}})\quad=Q

Which we shall write as:

\displaystyle Q=(\bm{\nu}z^{!}_{q},\cdots,z^{!}_{1})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}~{!}z^{!}_{1}(z_{1});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{z_{1}}\cdots\mathbin{|}~{!}z^{!}_{q}(z_{q});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{q}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{z_{q}})

for simplicity to represent the process. We also use this to simplify the translation of linear explicit substitutions of bags from:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdot\lbag M_{k}\rbag/x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}{{\displaystyle\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

to be represented as:

\begin{aligned} {{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}C/\widetilde{x}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}&=(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z})({\widetilde{z}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C)};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{\widetilde{z}}\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{\widetilde{x}_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(\widetilde{x})}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{z}/\widetilde{x}_{i}\}).\end{aligned}

These representations are purely for simplicity and are not an alternative to the actual translation. We continue expanding the sub-process

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}
using the above shortened and simplified notation:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{w}_{p})({\widetilde{w}_{p}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{p})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C_{p}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{\widetilde{w}_{p}}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{\widetilde{y}_{p_{i}}\in\mathsf{PER}(\widetilde{y}_{p})}\cdots(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{w}_{1})({\widetilde{w}_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{\widetilde{w}_{1}}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{\widetilde{y}_{1_{i}}\in\mathsf{PER}(\widetilde{y}_{1})}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{{1_{i}}}\})\cdots\{\widetilde{w}_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{{p_{i}}}\})

Hence we represent
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}
as:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z^{!}_{q},\cdots,z^{!}_{1})((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{w}_{p})({\widetilde{w}_{p}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{p})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C_{p}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{\widetilde{w}_{p}}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{\widetilde{y}_{p_{i}}\in\mathsf{PER}(\widetilde{y}_{p})}\cdots(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{w}_{1})({\widetilde{w}_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{\widetilde{w}_{1}}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{\widetilde{y}_{1_{i}}\in\mathsf{PER}(\widetilde{y}_{1})}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{{1_{i}}}\})\cdots\{\widetilde{w}_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{{p_{i}}}\})

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}~{!}z^{!}_{1}(z_{1});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{z_{1}}\cdots\mathbin{|}~{!}z^{!}_{q}(z_{q});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U_{q}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{z_{q}})

Finally we shall simplify the process to become:

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}=(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})\\

With this shape for
M
, we then have the following:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M(C\star U){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}\overline{v}.\mathtt{some};v(x);\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}v)((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}v(x);\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}v)([v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x)((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=Q_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x)((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x})

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

We also have that

\displaystyle N

\displaystyle=(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil(C\star U)

\displaystyle\equiv_{\lambda}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])(C\star U)){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil

\displaystyle\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\star U)$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil={M}

Furthermore, we have:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\star U)$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)((\bm{\nu}\widetilde{z},\widetilde{w})(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{\widetilde{w}/\widetilde{y}_{i}\}\mathbin{|}Q^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}

We consider different possibilities for
n\geq 1
; in all the cases, the result follows.

When
n=1
:

We have
Q=Q_{1}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{1}Q_{1}
.
We also have that

•

Q_{1}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{2}Q_{3}=Q^{\prime}
,

•

{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{1}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\star U)$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil={N}^{\prime}

•

and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(C\star U)$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q_{3}
.

When
n=2
:
the analysis is similar.

When
n\geq 3
:

We have
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{3}Q_{3}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{l}Q
, for
l\geq 0
. We also know that
{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}{M}
,
Q_{3}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
. By the IH, there exist
Q^{\prime},{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}Q^{\prime}
,
{M}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{N}^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Finally,
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{3}Q_{3}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{l}Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}Q^{\prime}
and
{N}\rightarrow{M}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{N}^{\prime}
.

B.

M=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}
.

Then,
\begin{aligned} {{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}&={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}=\overline{v}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\widetilde{z}}.\mathtt{none}.\end{aligned}

With this shape for
M
, we have:

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(M\ (C\star U)){\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)(\overline{v}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\widetilde{z}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}\overline{\widetilde{z}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{u}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\mathsf{lfv}(C)}.\mathtt{none}

We also have that

{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\ C\star U\mathbin{\longrightarrow}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)}={M}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\cup\mathsf{lfv}(C)}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=\overline{\widetilde{z}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{u}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}\overline{\mathsf{lfv}(C)}.\mathtt{none}

ii.

When
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
, we distinguish two cases:

A.

When
n=0
:

Then,
(\bm{\nu}v)(R\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u])))=Q
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{m}R
where
m\geq 1
. Then by the IH there exist
R^{\prime}
and
{M}^{\prime}
such that
R\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}R^{\prime}
,
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{M}^{\prime}
, and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=R^{\prime}
. Hence we have that

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}v)({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{m}(\bm{\nu}v)(R\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))=Q

We also know that

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}(\bm{\nu}v)(R\mathbin{|}{v.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{v}[x];({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x}\mathbin{|}[v\mathbin{\leftrightarrow}u]))=Q^{\prime}

and so the
\lambda_{\mathtt{C}}
term can reduce as follows:
{N}=(M\ (C\star U))\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}M^{\prime}\ (C\star U)={N}^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

B.

When
n\geq 1
:

Then
R
has an occurrence of an unguarded
\overline{v}.\mathtt{some}
or
\overline{v}.\mathtt{none}
, hence it is of the form

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}(\lambda x.(M^{\prime}[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}])){\langle\!|}C_{1}/\widetilde{y}_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}C_{p}/\widetilde{y}_{p}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U_{1}/z^{!}_{1}\rrceil\cdots\llfloor U_{q}/z^{!}_{q}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}
or
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}.

This case follows by IH.

This concludes the analysis for the case
{N}=(M\,(C\star U))
.

3.

{N}=M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]
.

The sharing variable
{x}
is not free and the result follows by vacuity.

4.

{N}=M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Then we have

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}x)(\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}C\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x})

Let us consider three cases.

(a)

When
\mathsf{size}({\widetilde{x}})=\mathsf{size}(C)
.
Then let us consider the shape of the bag
C
.

i.

When
C=\mathtt{1}
.

We have the following

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)(\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}\star U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x)(\overline{x}.\mathtt{some};x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(C)};\overline{x}[x^{\ell}];\big({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[x^{!}];({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[])\big))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x)(x(x^{\ell});x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[x^{\ell}];\big({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[x^{!}];({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[])\big))

\displaystyle=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x)(\overline{x}[x^{!}];({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}\overline{x}[])\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{\ell})(x(x^{!});x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}}))

\displaystyle=Q_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x)(\overline{x}[]\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{\ell})(x();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}})))

\displaystyle=Q_{3}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{\ell})({{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow{x}]{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x^{\ell}})))

\displaystyle=Q_{4}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{\ell})(\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{some};\overline{x^{\ell}}[y_{i}];({y_{i}.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{none})\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}}_{\emptyset};x(y_{i});(\overline{y_{i}}.\mathtt{some};\overline{y_{i}}[]\mathbin{|}{x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}}_{\emptyset};\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{none}))))

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{\ell})(\overline{x^{\ell}}[y_{i}];({y_{i}.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{none})\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle x(y_{i});(\overline{y_{i}}.\mathtt{some};\overline{y_{i}}[]\mathbin{|}{x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}}_{\emptyset};\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{none}))))

\displaystyle=Q_{5}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}x^{\ell})(\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{none}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}y_{i})({y_{i}.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}

\displaystyle\overline{y_{i}}.\mathtt{some};\overline{y_{i}}[]\mathbin{|}{x^{\ell}.\mathtt{some}}_{\emptyset};\overline{x^{\ell}}.\mathtt{none})))

\displaystyle=Q_{6}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}y_{i})({y_{i}.\mathtt{some}}_{u,\mathsf{lfv}(M)};y_{i}();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}\overline{y_{i}}.\mathtt{some};\overline{y_{i}}[]))

\displaystyle=Q_{7}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}(\bm{\nu}y_{i})(\ y_{i}();{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{|}\overline{y_{i}}[]))

\displaystyle=Q_{8}

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}(\bm{\nu}x^{!})({!}x^{!}(x_{i});{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}U{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{x_{i}}\mathbin{|}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u})={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=Q_{9}

Notice how
Q_{6}
has a choice however the
x^{\ell}
name can be closed at any time so for simplicity we perform communication across this name first followed by all other comunications that can take place.

Now we proceed by induction on the number of reductions
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{k}Q
.

A.

When
k=0
, the result follows trivially. Just take
{N}={N}^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q=Q^{\prime}
.

B.

When
k=1
.

We have
Q=Q_{1}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{1}Q_{1}

We also have that
Q_{1}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{8}Q_{9}=Q^{\prime}
,
{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil=N^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q_{9}

C.

When
2\leq k\leq 8
.

Proceeds similarly to the previous case

D.

When
k\geq 9
.

We have
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{9}Q_{9}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{l}Q
, for
l\geq 0
. Since
Q_{9}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
we apply the induction hypothesis we have that there exist
Q^{\prime},{N}^{\prime}\ s.t.\ Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}Q^{\prime},M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{N}^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Then,
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{5}Q_{5}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{l}Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}Q^{\prime}
and by the contextual reduction rule it follows that
{N}=(M[\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$1$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{N}^{\prime}
and the case holds.

ii.

When
C=\lbag N_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdot\lbag N_{l}\rbag
, for
l\geq 1
.
Then,

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M[{\widetilde{x}}\leftarrow{x}]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$C\star U$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

The proof follows by induction on the number of reductions
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{k}Q
.

A.

When
k=0
, the result follows trivially. Just take
{N}={N}^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q=Q^{\prime}
.

B.

When
1\leq k\leq 6l+9
.

Let
Q_{k}
such that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{k}Q_{k}
.
We also have that
Q_{k}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{6l+9-k}Q_{6l+9}=Q^{\prime}
,

{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{1}M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdots\lbag M_{l}\rbag/{x_{1}},\cdots,x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil={N}^{\prime}
and

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdots\lbag M_{l}\rbag/{x_{1}},\cdots,x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q_{6l+9}
.

C.

When
k>6l+9
.

Then,
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{6l+9}Q_{6l+9}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{n}Q
for
n\geq 1
. Also,

\begin{aligned} &{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{1}M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdots\lbag M_{l}\rbag/{x_{1}},\cdots,x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\text{ and }\\
&Q_{6l+9}={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdots\lbag M_{l}\rbag/{x_{1}},\cdots,x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}.\end{aligned}

By the induction hypothesis, there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
{N}^{\prime}
such that

\ Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}Q^{\prime}
,

M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdots\lbag M_{l}\rbag/{x_{1}},\cdots,x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{N}^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

Finally,
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{N}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{6l+9}Q_{6l+9}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{n}Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}^{i}Q^{\prime}
and

{N}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M{\langle\!|}\lbag M_{1}\rbag\cdot\cdots\cdots\lbag M_{l}\rbag/{x_{1}},\cdots,x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\llfloor U/x^{!}\rrceil\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{j}{N}^{\prime}.
