,

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}C/x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=

\displaystyle(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}{{\displaystyle\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

Therefore,

\displaystyle{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{m}(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}R\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

\displaystyle\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{n}Q

for some process

. Where
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{n}
is a reduction that initially synchronizes with
{{x}_{i}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{J_{i}})}
for some
i\in\{1,\cdots,k\}
, when

,
n+m=k\geq 1
. Type preservation in
{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
ensures reducing
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{v}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{m}
does not consume possible synchronizations with
\overline{{x}_{i}}.\mathtt{some}
, if they occur. Let us consider the the possible sizes of both

and
n
.

(a)

For
m=0
and
n\geq 1
.

We have that
R={{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
as
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{0}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
.

Notice that there are two possibilities of having an unguarded
\overline{x_{i}}.\mathtt{some}
or
\overline{x_{i}}.\mathtt{none}
without internal reductions:

i.

.

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

\displaystyle=(\bm{\nu}z_{1})({z_{1}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{1})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{1}}}\mathbin{|}\cdots(\bm{\nu}z_{k})({z_{k}.\mathtt{some}}_{\mathsf{lfv}(M_{k})};{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{{z_{k}}}

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{{x}_{i},\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\{z_{1}/x_{i_{1}}\}\cdots\{z_{k}/x_{i_{k}}\})\cdots)

\displaystyle\mathbin{|}\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{1}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}}\cdots\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{x_{i_{k}}\in\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\setminus x_{i_{1}},\cdots,x_{i_{k-1}}\}}

0.H.3
Success Sensitivity

Theorem 0.H.1

(Success Sensitivity (Under
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}
))

{{M}}\Downarrow{{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\lambda}}
iff
{{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\Downarrow}}{{\checkmark\mkern-4.0mu}_{\pi}}
for well-formed closed terms
M
.

Proof.

We proceed with the proof in two parts.

1.

Suppose that
{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\Downarrow}}_{\checkmark}
.

By Def.
17
, there exists
{M}^{\prime}
such that
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{*}{M}^{\prime}
and

\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=\checkmark
.
By completeness, if
M\mathbin{\longrightarrow}M^{\prime}
then there exist
Q,Q^{\prime}
such that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\equiv Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}Q^{\prime}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\succeq_{\mkern-2.0mu\scalebox{.8}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}$}}Q^{\prime}
.

We wish to show that there exists
Q^{\prime\prime}
such that
Q^{\prime}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}Q^{\prime\prime}
and
Q^{\prime\prime}
has an unguarded occurrence of
\checkmark
.

By Proposition
4
(1) we have that
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=\checkmark\implies{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{x})(P\mathbin{|}\checkmark)
. Finally
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{x})(P\mathbin{|}\checkmark)\succeq_{\mkern-2.0mu\scalebox{.8}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}$}}Q^{\prime}
hence
Q
must be of the form
(\bm{\nu}\widetilde{x})(P^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\checkmark)
where
P\succeq_{\mkern-2.0mu\scalebox{.8}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}$}}P^{\prime}
. Hence
Q
reduces to a process that has an unguarded occurence of
\checkmark
.

2.

Suppose that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\Downarrow}}_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By operational soundness we have that if
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}Q
then there exist
{M}^{\prime}
and
\{Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}
such that:
(i)
{M}\mathbin{\longrightarrow}^{*}{M}^{\prime}

and
(ii)
Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}\{Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}
with

{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\succeq_{\mkern-2.0mu\scalebox{.8}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}$}}Q_{j}
, for all
j\in I
.

Since
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}P_{1}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\ldots\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}P_{k}(:=Q)
, and
P_{j}=P_{j}^{\prime}\mathbin{|}\checkmark
and
Q\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{*}\{Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}
we must have that each
Q_{i}
is of the form
P^{\prime}_{i_{1}}\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\ldots\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}P^{\prime}_{i_{k}}
with
P^{\prime}_{i_{j}}=P^{\prime\prime}_{i_{j}}\mathbin{|}\checkmark
. As
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}\succeq_{\mkern-2.0mu\scalebox{.8}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}$}}Q_{j}
, for all
j\in I
we have that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}=(\scalebox{1.5}{$\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\mkern-3.0mu$}_{i\in I}Q_{i})\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}R
for some
R
. Finally applying Proposition
4
(2) we have that
{{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\llbracket}{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.85,0,0.42}\rrbracket}}_{u}
is itself a term with unguarded
\checkmark
, then
{M}
is itself headed with
\checkmark
.
∎

Appendix 0.I
Proof of Separation of Lazy and Eager Semantics

Definition 19

(Dual Prefix)
.

Given prefixes
\alpha
and
\beta
(
Definition

2
), we say
\alpha
and
\beta
are duals, denoted
\alpha\mathbin{\overline{\bowtie}}\beta
, if and only if

\mathit{subj}\{\alpha\}\cap\mathit{subj}\{\beta\}\neq\emptyset
.

Lemma 18

Given
P\vdash\emptyset
, if
P\downarrow_{\alpha}
, then there exist
P^{\prime},\beta
such that
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}P^{\prime}\downarrow_{\beta}
and
\alpha\mathbin{\overline{\bowtie}}\beta
.

Proof.

By well-typedness, there appears
\beta
in
P
with
\alpha\mathbin{\overline{\bowtie}}\beta
.
However, we may have
P\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.5pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\downarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.5pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\downarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.04861pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\downarrow}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.84029pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\downarrow}}}_{\mkern-7.0mu\beta}
, because
\beta
is blocked by other prefixes.
Hence, we need to find reductions from
P
such that we unblock
\beta
.
However, the prefixes blocking
\beta
are connected to dual prefixes, that may be blocked themselves.
The crux of this proof is thus to show that we can reduce
P
, such that we eventually unblock
\beta
.

The proof is by induction on the number of names that may block
\beta
(IH
1
).
Initially, this number corresponds to the total number of names appearing in
P
.
Suppose
\beta
is blocked by
n
prefixes
\gamma_{i}
, where
\gamma_{n}
blocks
\beta
, and
\gamma_{1}
is not blocked.
We apply another layer of induction on
n
(IH
2
).

In the inductive case,
n\geq 1
.
The goal is to perform a reduction that synchronizes
\gamma_{1}
with its dual, say
\overline{\gamma_{1}}
.
The prefix
\overline{\gamma_{1}}
may be blocked by a number of prefixes itself.
However, the type system of
{{\mathsf{s}\pi\mkern-2.0mu}^{+}\mkern-1.0mu}
is based on
Cut
, so
\overline{\gamma_{1}}
appears in parallel with the duals of
\gamma_{2},\ldots,\gamma_{n}
and
\alpha
.
We then may apply IH
1
to find
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}P_{0}\downarrow_{\overline{\gamma_{1}}}
.
We can then reduce
P_{0}
by synchronizing between
\gamma_{1}
and
\overline{\gamma_{1}}
:
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}P_{0}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}P_{1}
.
In
P_{1}
,
\beta
is blocked by one less prefix.
Hence, by IH
2
,
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}P_{0}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}P_{1}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}P^{\prime}\downarrow_{\beta}
, proving the thesis.

In the base case,
\beta
is not blocked:
P\downarrow_{\beta}
.
Let
P^{\prime}:=P
; trivially,
P\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}P^{\prime}\downarrow_{\beta}
, proving the thesis.
∎

See
0.B.5

Proof.

For (i), we construct a relation
\mathbb{B}
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Id}^{\equiv}

\displaystyle:=\{(T,U)\mid T\equiv U\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\{(T,U)\mid\begin{array}[t]{@{}l@{}}T\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};(V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}W){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\emptyset\text{ and}\\
U\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{2};V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\beta_{3};W{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\vdash\emptyset\text{ and}\\
V\not\sim_{\mkern-1.0mu\mathtt{L}}W\text{ and}\\
\beta_{1}\bowtie\beta_{2}\bowtie\beta_{3}\text{ and}\\
\text{$\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}$ require a continuation}\}\end{array}

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{Id}^{\equiv}\cup\mathbb{B}^{\prime}

We prove that
\mathbb{B}
is a strong ready-prefix bisimulation w.r.t. the lazy semantics by proving the three conditions of
Definition

9
for each
(T,U)\in\mathbb{B}
.
We distinguish cases depending on whether
(T,U)\in\mathsf{Id}^{\equiv}
or
(T,U)\in\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
.

•

(T,U)\in\mathsf{Id}^{\equiv}
.
The three conditions hold trivially.

•

(T,U)\in\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
.
Then
T\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};(V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}W){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
,
U\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{2};V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\beta_{3};W{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
,
V\not\sim_{\mkern-1.0mu\mathtt{L}}W
, and
\beta_{1}\bowtie\beta_{2}\bowtie\beta_{3}
.
We prove each condition separately.

1.

Suppose
T\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}T^{\prime}
.
Note that the hole in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
may appear inside a non-deterministic choice.
We distinguish three cases: (a) the reduction is inside
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and maintains the branch with the hole, (b) the reduction is inside
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and discards the branch with the hole, or (c) the reduction synchronizes on
\beta_{1}
.

(a)

The reduction is inside
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and maintains the branch with the hole.
Then
T^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};(V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}W){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and
U\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}U^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{2};V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\beta_{3};W{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
Clearly,
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathbb{B}^{\prime}
, so
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathbb{B}
.

(b)

The reduction is inside
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
and discards the branch with the hole.
Then there exists
U^{\prime}
such that
U\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}U^{\prime}\equiv T^{\prime}
, so
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Id}^{\equiv}
, and thus
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathbb{B}
.

(c)

The reduction synchronizes on
\beta_{1}
.
Then
T^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}W{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
and, since
\beta_{1}\bowtie\beta_{2}\bowtie\beta_{3}
,
U\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}U^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}V\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}W{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
Then
T^{\prime}\equiv U^{\prime}
, so
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Id}^{\equiv}
and thus
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathbb{B}
.

2.

Suppose
U\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}U^{\prime}
.
By reasoning analogous to above,
T\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.38,0.61,0.27}\leadsto}}T^{\prime}
and
(T^{\prime},U^{\prime})\in\mathbb{B}
.

3.

Suppose
T\downarrow_{\gamma}
.
If the prefix
\gamma
appears in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{M}}
, then clearly also
U\downarrow_{\gamma}
.
Otherwise,
\gamma=\beta_{1}
.
We have, e.g.,
\gamma\bowtie\beta_{2}
and clearly
U\downarrow_{\beta_{2}}
.
The other direction is analogous.

It remains to show that
(R,S)\in\mathbb{B}
which trivially holds.

For (ii), toward a contradiction, assume there exists a strong ready-prefix bisimulation w.r.t.
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}

\mathbb{B}
where
(R,S)\in\mathbb{B}
.

By
Lemma

18
, there exist
R^{\prime},\beta_{1}
such that
R\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}R^{\prime}\downarrow_{\beta_{1}}
, and
\alpha_{1}\mathbin{\overline{\bowtie}}\beta_{1}
.
By the well-typedness of
R
and
S
,
\beta_{1}
must appear in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}
, and the reduction
R\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}R^{\prime}
takes place in
{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N}}
.
Take
x\in\mathit{subj}\{\alpha_{1}\}\cap\mathit{subj}\{\beta_{1}\}
(which is non-empty by
Lemma

18
).
Then
R^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};R^{\prime}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}_{3}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{1};(P\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}
.
Moreover, clearly
S\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}^{\ast}S^{\prime}
following the same reductions, resulting in
S^{\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}_{1}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big[}(\bm{\nu}x)({\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}_{2}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\beta_{1};R^{\prime}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}\mathbin{|}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime}_{3}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}\alpha_{2};P\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}\alpha_{3};Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}){\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\big]}
; note that, since
\alpha_{1}\bowtie\alpha_{2}\bowtie\alpha_{3}
, also
\alpha_{2}\mathbin{\overline{\bowtie}}\beta_{1}
and
\alpha_{3}\mathbin{\overline{\bowtie}}\beta_{1}
.
At this point, we must have
(R^{\prime},S^{\prime})\in\mathbb{B}
.

The synchronization between
\beta_{1}
and
\alpha_{1}
gives
R^{\prime}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}R^{\prime\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}P\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
Then by the bisimulation, there exists
S^{\prime\prime}
such that
S^{\prime}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}S^{\prime\prime}
with
(R^{\prime\prime},S^{\prime\prime})\in\mathbb{B}
.
By clause 3 of the bisimulation,
R^{\prime\prime}
and
S^{\prime\prime}
must have the same ready-prefixes, so clearly the reduction
S^{\prime}\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}S^{\prime\prime}
results from a synchronization between
\beta_{1}
and either of
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{3}
.
W.l.o.g., let us assume this was
\alpha_{3}
.
Then
S^{\prime\prime}\equiv{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}\mathtt{N^{\prime\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}[}Q{\color[rgb]{0,0.56,0.61}]}
.
By assumption,
P\not\sim_{\mkern-1.0mu\mathtt{E}}Q
and thus
P\mathbin{\hbox{\hbox to0.0pt{$\mkern-0.4mu{}\mathbin{|\mkern-3.0mu|}{}$\hss}$-$}}Q\not\sim_{\mkern-1.0mu\mathtt{E}}Q
, so clearly
R^{\prime\prime}\not\sim_{\mkern-1.0mu\mathtt{E}}S^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence,
\mathbb{B}
cannot be a strong ready-prefix bisimulation w.r.t.
\mathbin{{\color[rgb]{0.6,0,0.01}\longrightarrow}}
.
In other words,
R\not\sim_{\mkern-1.0mu\mathtt{E}}S
.
∎
